to write,

\displaystyle W_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{V_{P}}d^{3}x\,\sqrt{g}\left(\xi^{\psi}\left(J^{\vartheta}Q^{\varphi}-J^{\varphi}Q^{\vartheta}\right)+Q^{\psi}\left(\xi^{\alpha}J^{\varphi}-p^{\prime}(\psi)\xi^{\varphi}\right)\right).

(P.5)

The third term in the energy functional can be expressed as,

\displaystyle W_{3}

\displaystyle\equiv\int_{V_{P}}d^{3}x\,\bm{\xi}\cdot\nabla\left(\bm{\xi}\cdot\nabla p\right)

(P.6)

\displaystyle=\int_{V_{P}}d^{3}x\,\left[\xi^{\psi}\frac{\partial(\xi^{\psi}p^{\prime}(\psi))}{\partial\psi}+p^{\prime}(\psi)\left(\xi^{\alpha}\frac{\partial\xi^{\psi}}{\partial\vartheta}+\sqrt{g}Q^{\psi}\xi^{\varphi}\right)\right].

Combining
W_{2}
and
W_{3}
, we see that the energy functional indeed only depends on
\xi^{\alpha}
and
\xi^{\psi}
,

W_{2}+W_{3}=\int_{V_{P}}d^{3}x\,\Bigg(\sqrt{g}\xi^{\psi}\left(J^{\vartheta}Q^{\varphi}-J^{\varphi}Q^{\vartheta}\right)+\xi^{\alpha}\textbf{J}\cdot\nabla\xi^{\psi}+\xi^{\psi}\frac{\partial(\xi^{\psi}p^{\prime}(\psi))}{\partial\psi}\Bigg).

(P.7)

We now can apply the divegernce theorem, noting that
\nabla\cdot\textbf{J}=\textbf{J}\cdot\nabla\psi=0
, to obtain,

\displaystyle W_{2}+W_{3}=\int_{V_{P}}d^{3}x\,\Bigg(\xi^{\psi}\left(J^{\varphi}\iota^{\prime}(\psi)\xi^{\psi}-2\textbf{J}\cdot\nabla\xi^{\alpha}+\xi^{\psi}p^{\prime\prime}(\psi)\right)\Bigg).

(P.8)

We now see that the first three terms of the energy functional only depend on
\xi^{\alpha}
through its
\vartheta
and
\varphi
derivatives. Furthermore, given the restriction of
\delta F_{\alpha}
discussed in Appendix
Q
, the
m=0
,
n=0
mode of
\xi^{\alpha}
will not enter the variational principle.

P.2
Explicit forms of coefficient matrices

We can now express the linear operators that couple the Fourier components of
\xi^{\alpha}
,
\xi^{\psi}
, and
\partial\xi^{\psi}/\partial\psi
given the simplifications of the energy functional in the previous Section:

\displaystyle\textbf{A}_{\psi^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{V^{\prime}(\psi)}{\mu_{0}}\left\langle\frac{1}{\left(\sqrt{g}\right)^{2}}\left(g_{\varphi\varphi}+\iota(\psi)^{2}g_{\vartheta\vartheta}\right)\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\psi}\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\psi}\right\rangle_{\psi}

(P.9a)

\displaystyle\textbf{A}_{\psi\psi}

\displaystyle=\frac{V^{\prime}(\psi)}{\mu_{0}}\Bigg\langle\frac{1}{\left(\sqrt{g}\right)^{2}}\Bigg[-g_{\psi\psi}\Bigg(\frac{\partial\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\psi}}{\partial\varphi}\frac{\partial\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\psi}}{\partial\varphi}+\iota(\psi)^{2}\frac{\partial\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\psi}}{\partial\vartheta}\frac{\partial\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\psi}}{\partial\vartheta}\Bigg)

(P.9b)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-g_{\psi\psi}\iota(\psi)\left(\frac{\partial\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\psi}}{\partial\vartheta}\frac{\partial\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\psi}}{\partial\varphi}+\frac{\partial\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\psi}}{\partial\varphi}\frac{\partial\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\psi}}{\partial\vartheta}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\left(\mu_{0}\left(\sqrt{g}\right)^{2}\left(J^{\varphi}\iota^{\prime}(\psi)+p^{\prime\prime}(\psi)\right)-g_{\vartheta\vartheta}\left(\iota^{\prime}(\psi)\right)^{2}\right)\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\psi}\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\psi}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+g_{\psi\vartheta}\iota^{\prime}(\psi)\left(\left(\frac{\partial\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\psi}}{\partial\varphi}+\iota(\psi)\frac{\partial\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\psi}}{\partial\vartheta}\right)\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\psi}+\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\psi}\left(\frac{\partial\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\psi}}{\partial\varphi}+\iota(\psi)\frac{\partial\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\psi}}{\partial\vartheta}\right)\right)\Bigg]\Bigg\rangle_{\psi}

\displaystyle\textbf{A}_{\psi\psi^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{V^{\prime}(\psi)}{\mu_{0}}\left\langle\frac{2\iota(\psi)}{\left(\sqrt{g}\right)^{2}}\Bigg[-\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\psi}g_{\vartheta\vartheta}\iota^{\prime}(\psi)+g_{\psi\vartheta}\left(\frac{\partial\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\psi}}{\partial\varphi}+\iota(\psi)\frac{\partial\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\psi}}{\partial\vartheta}\right)\Bigg]\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\psi}\right\rangle_{\psi}

(P.9c)

\displaystyle\textbf{A}_{\alpha\alpha}

\displaystyle=-\frac{V^{\prime}(\psi)}{\mu_{0}}\left\langle\frac{1}{\left(\sqrt{g}\right)^{2}}\Bigg[g_{\vartheta\vartheta}\frac{\partial\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\alpha}}{\partial\varphi}\frac{\partial\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\alpha}}{\partial\varphi}+g_{\varphi\varphi}\frac{\partial\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\alpha}}{\partial\vartheta}\frac{\partial\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\alpha}}{\partial\vartheta}\Bigg]\right\rangle_{\psi}

(P.9d)

\displaystyle\textbf{A}_{\alpha\psi^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2V^{\prime}(\psi)}{\mu_{0}}\left\langle\,\frac{1}{\left(\sqrt{g}\right)^{2}}\Bigg[g_{\vartheta\vartheta}\iota\frac{\partial\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\alpha}}{\partial\varphi}-g_{\varphi\varphi}\frac{\partial\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\alpha}}{\partial\vartheta}\Bigg]\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\psi}\right\rangle_{\psi}

(P.9e)

\displaystyle\textbf{A}_{\alpha\psi}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2V^{\prime}(\psi)}{\mu_{0}}\Bigg\langle\frac{1}{\left(\sqrt{g}\right)^{2}}\Bigg[\left(-g_{\vartheta\vartheta}\iota^{\prime}(\psi)\frac{\partial\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\alpha}}{\partial\varphi}+\mu_{0}\left(\sqrt{g}\right)^{2}\textbf{J}\cdot\nabla\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\alpha}\right)\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\psi}

(P.9f)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+g_{\psi\vartheta}\frac{\partial\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\alpha}}{\partial\varphi}\left(\frac{\partial\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\psi}}{\partial\varphi}+\iota(\psi)\frac{\partial\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\psi}}{\partial\vartheta}\right)\Bigg]\Bigg\rangle_{\psi}

\displaystyle\textbf{I}_{\psi}

\displaystyle=2V^{\prime}(\psi)\left\langle\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\psi}\delta F_{\psi}\right\rangle_{\psi}

(P.9g)

\displaystyle\textbf{I}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=2V^{\prime}(\psi)\left\langle\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{\alpha}\delta F_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\psi},

(P.9h)

where
\left\langle...\right\rangle_{\psi}
is the flux-surface average (
A.10
).

P.3
Invertibility of
\textbf{A}_{\alpha\alpha}

Obtaining the Euler-Lagrange solution for
\xi^{\alpha}
requires inverting
\textbf{A}_{\alpha\alpha}
. We now show that this matrix is, in fact, negative definite and thus invertible. For any non-zero vector
\bm{\Xi}_{\alpha}
, we can write the inner product with
\textbf{A}_{\alpha\alpha}
as,

\displaystyle\bm{\Xi}_{\alpha}\cdot\left(\textbf{A}_{\alpha\alpha}\bm{\Xi}_{\alpha}\right)=-\frac{1}{\mu_{0}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}d\vartheta\int_{0}^{2\pi}d\varphi\,\left[\frac{g_{\vartheta\vartheta}}{\sqrt{g}}\left(\frac{\partial\xi^{\alpha}}{\partial\varphi}\right)^{2}+\frac{g_{\varphi\varphi}}{\sqrt{g}}\left(\frac{\partial\xi^{\alpha}}{\partial\vartheta}\right)^{2}\right].

(P.10)

We note that for a well-defined coordinate system,
g_{\vartheta\vartheta}>0
,
g_{\varphi\varphi}>0
, and
\sqrt{g}>0
. While either
\partial\xi^{\alpha}/\partial\varphi
or
\partial\xi^{\alpha}/\partial\vartheta
may vanish, they will not vanish simultaneously throughout the integrand as we have excluded the
n=0
,
m=0
mode. Therefore, the integrand will only vanish at isolated points. Thus the above integral is negative definite, and
\textbf{A}_{\alpha\alpha}
is invertible throughout the volume.

Appendix Q:   Constraint on bulk force perturbation

As shown in Appendix
P
, the first three terms in the energy functional (
6.11
) only depend on
\xi^{\alpha}
through its derivatives with respect to
\vartheta
and
\varphi
. In this Appendix, we show that it is always possible to choose the in-surface component of the bulk force perturbation,
\delta F_{\alpha}
, such that the final term in the energy functional,

\displaystyle W_{4}\equiv\int_{V_{P}}d^{3}x\,\xi^{\alpha}\delta F_{\alpha},

(Q.1)

does not depend on
\xi^{\alpha c}_{0,0}=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}d\vartheta\int_{0}^{2\pi}d\varphi\,\xi^{\alpha}
. As
\xi^{\alpha c}_{0,0}
does not enter our variational principle, we can take it to vanish.
The condition that
\xi^{\alpha c}_{0,0}
does not enter
W_{4}
is equivalent to requiring that,

\displaystyle\langle\delta F_{\alpha}\rangle_{\psi}=0,

(Q.2)

on every surface, where
\langle\dots\rangle_{\psi}
is the flux-surface average (
A.10
). This follows from the surface-averaged in-surface component of the linearized force-balance equation (
6.2
),

\displaystyle\left\langle\frac{\partial\textbf{x}}{\partial\vartheta}\cdot\textbf{F}[\bm{\xi}]\right\rangle_{\psi}=0.

(Q.3)

This property of the MHD force operator holds for any equilibrium field that satisfies MHD force balance (
6.1
). To see this we note that the flux-surface average can be defined in terms of an average over the infinitesimal volume between flux surfaces
\Delta V
(
A.12
). We can now apply the self-adjointness relation (
6.9
) to simplify (
Q.3
),

\left\langle\frac{\partial\textbf{x}}{\partial\vartheta}\cdot\textbf{F}[\bm{\xi}]\right\rangle_{\psi}=\left\langle\bm{\xi}\cdot\textbf{F}\left[\frac{\partial\textbf{x}}{\partial\vartheta}\right]\right\rangle_{\psi}\\
+\lim_{\Delta V\rightarrow 0}\frac{1}{\mu_{0}\Delta V}\left(\int_{\partial(V_{P}+\Delta V)}d^{2}x\,\hat{\textbf{n}}\cdot\bm{\xi}\textbf{B}\cdot\delta\textbf{B}\left[\frac{\partial\textbf{x}}{\partial\vartheta}\right]-\int_{\partial(V_{P})}d^{2}x\,\hat{\textbf{n}}\cdot\bm{\xi}\textbf{B}\cdot\delta\textbf{B}\left[\frac{\partial\textbf{x}}{\partial\vartheta}\right]\right),

(Q.4)

where we have noted that
\hat{\textbf{n}}\cdot\frac{\partial\textbf{x}}{\partial\vartheta}=0
, as
\hat{\textbf{n}}\propto\nabla\psi
. The quantity
\delta\textbf{B}\left[\partial\textbf{x}/\partial\vartheta\right]=\nabla\times\left(\partial\textbf{x}/\partial\vartheta\times\textbf{B}\right)
is shown to vanish by expressing
B
in contravariant form and using the dual relations (
A.3
) between the contravariant and covariant basis vectors. The remaining flux-surface averaged term can also be shown to vanish,

\left\langle\bm{\xi}\cdot\textbf{F}\left[\frac{\partial\textbf{x}}{\partial\vartheta}\right]\right\rangle_{\psi}=\\
\left\langle\bm{\xi}\cdot\left(\textbf{J}\times\delta\textbf{B}\left[\frac{\partial\textbf{x}}{\partial\vartheta}\right]+\frac{\left(\nabla\times\delta\textbf{B}\left[\frac{\partial\textbf{x}}{\partial\vartheta}\right]\right)\times\textbf{B}}{\mu_{0}}+\nabla\left(\frac{\partial\textbf{x}}{\partial\vartheta}\cdot\nabla p\right)\right)\right\rangle_{\psi},

(Q.5)

as
\frac{\partial\textbf{x}}{\partial\varphi}\cdot\nabla\psi=0
and
\delta\textbf{B}\left[\partial\textbf{x}/\partial\vartheta\right]=0
.

Therefore, we see that in order to satisfy linear force balance,
\delta F_{\alpha}
must be chosen to satisfy the condition (
Q.2
). However, this property can
always
be imparted on a bulk force arising from the adjoint formulation. Consider the fixed-boundary adjoint relation (
5.36
) without perturbations to the rotational transform,

\displaystyle\int_{V_{P}}d^{3}x\,\left(\bm{\xi}_{1}\cdot\textbf{F}_{2}-\bm{\xi}_{2}\cdot\textbf{F}_{1}\right)-\frac{1}{\mu_{0}}\int_{S_{P}}d^{2}x\,\hat{\textbf{n}}\cdot\left(\bm{\xi}_{2}\delta\textbf{B}[\bm{\xi}_{1}]\cdot\textbf{B}-\bm{\xi}_{1}\delta\textbf{B}[\bm{\xi}_{2}]\cdot\textbf{B}\right)=0.

(Q.6)

As
\delta\textbf{B}[\bm{\xi}]
does not depend on
\xi^{\alpha c}_{0,0}
, we can choose to define the displacement vector such that
\xi^{\alpha c}_{0,0}=0
. This is analogous to our convention that
\bm{\xi}\cdot\textbf{B}=0
, as
\delta\textbf{B}[\bm{\xi}]
does not depend on the parallel component of
\bm{\xi}
. Given this convention for the displacement vector, we can note that
\langle\delta F_{\alpha,2}\rangle_{\psi}
and
\langle\delta F_{\alpha,1}\rangle_{\psi}
do not enter the above adjoint relation. Therefore, we are free to choose our bulk force such that the desired constraint (
Q.2
) is satisfied.

Appendix R:   Near-axis expansion of screw pinch equilibria

The MHD force-balance equation for a screw pinch is,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dr}\left(\mu_{0}p(r)+\frac{1}{2r^{2}}\left(\psi^{\prime}(r)\right)^{2}\right)+\frac{\iota(r)\psi^{\prime}(r)}{R_{0}^{2}r}\frac{d}{dr}\left(r\iota(r)\psi^{\prime}(r)\right)=0.

(R.1)

We note that (
R.1
) remains unchanged under the transformation
r\rightarrow-r
, so
\psi(r)
must be even in
r
. Thus near the origin we can express the flux function as,

\displaystyle\psi(r)=\frac{\psi_{2}}{2}r^{2}+\frac{\psi_{4}}{4!}r^{4}+\frac{\psi_{6}}{6!}r^{6}+\mathcal{O}(r^{8}),

(R.2)

under the assumption that
\psi(0)=0
. We similarly express the rotational transform and pressure profiles in a power series near the axis,

\displaystyle\iota(\psi(r))

\displaystyle=\iota_{0}+\iota_{1}\psi(r)+\frac{\iota_{2}}{2}\psi(r)^{2}+\frac{\iota_{3}}{3!}\psi(r)^{3}+\mathcal{O}(\psi(r)^{4})

(R.3a)

\displaystyle p(\psi(r))

\displaystyle=p_{0}+p_{1}\psi(r)+\frac{p_{2}}{2}\psi(r)^{2}+\frac{p_{3}}{3!}\psi(r)^{3}+\mathcal{O}(\psi(r)^{4}).

(R.3b)

The force-balance equation to
\mathcal{O}(r)
becomes,

\displaystyle\mu_{0}p_{1}\psi_{2}+\frac{2\iota_{0}^{2}\psi_{2}^{2}}{R_{0}^{2}}+\frac{\psi_{2}\psi_{4}}{3}=0,

(R.4)

and to
\mathcal{O}(r^{3})
it is,

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{0}p_{2}\psi_{2}^{2}}{2}+\frac{3\iota_{0}\iota_{1}\psi_{2}^{3}}{R_{0}^{2}}+\frac{\mu_{0}p_{1}\psi_{4}}{6}+\frac{\iota_{0}^{2}\psi_{2}\psi_{4}}{R_{0}^{2}}+\frac{\psi_{4}^{2}}{18}+\frac{\psi_{2}\psi_{6}}{30}=0.

(R.5)

In order to determine the power series expansion of
\psi
, we match the solution near the axis with a numerical solution for
\psi(r)
at some chosen boundary location near the axis,
r_{b}
. To perform an expansion to
\mathcal{O}(r^{2})
,
\psi_{2}
is chosen such that

\displaystyle\psi_{2}=\frac{2\psi(r_{b})}{r_{b}^{2}}.

(R.6)

To perform an expansion to
\mathcal{O}(r^{4})
, (
R.4
) is used to express
\psi_{4}
in terms of
\psi_{2}
, and
\psi_{2}
is chosen such that
\psi_{2}r_{b}^{2}/2+\psi_{4}r_{b}^{4}/4!=\psi(r_{b})
,

\displaystyle\psi_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{-\mu_{0}p_{1}r_{b}^{4}-8\psi(r_{b})}{2r_{b}^{2}\left(\frac{r_{b}^{2}\iota_{0}^{2}}{R_{0}^{2}}-2\right)}

(R.7a)

\displaystyle\psi_{4}

\displaystyle=-3\left(\mu_{0}p_{1}+\frac{2\iota_{0}^{2}\psi_{2}}{R_{0}^{2}}\right).

(R.7b)

To perform an expansion to
\mathcal{O}(r^{6})
, (
R.4
) and (
R.5
) are used to express
\psi_{4}
and
\psi_{6}
in terms of
\psi_{2}
, and
\psi_{2}
is chosen such that
\psi_{2}r_{b}^{2}/2+\psi_{4}r_{b}^{4}/4!+\psi_{6}r_{b}^{6}/6!=\psi(r_{b})
. The resulting equation for
\psi_{2}
is quadratic, but only one solution is allowed in practice to ensure that
(\psi_{6}r_{b}^{6}/6!)/(\psi_{2}r_{b}^{2}/2+\psi_{4}r_{b}^{4}/4!)\sim r_{b}^{2}
in the limit that
r_{b}\ll 1
,

\displaystyle\psi_{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{R_{0}^{2}}{12\iota_{0}\iota_{1}r_{b}^{6}}\Bigg(-24r_{b}^{2}+\frac{12r_{b}^{4}\iota_{0}^{2}}{R_{0}^{2}}+r_{b}^{6}\left(\mu_{0}p_{2}-\frac{8\iota_{0}^{4}}{R_{0}^{4}}\right)

(R.8a)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+r_{b}^{2}\Bigg[\left(-24+\mu_{0}p_{2}r_{b}^{4}+\frac{12r_{b}^{2}\iota_{0}^{2}}{R_{0}^{2}}-\frac{8r_{b}^{4}\iota_{0}^{4}}{R_{0}^{4}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{48r_{b}^{2}\iota_{0}\iota_{1}}{R_{0}^{2}}\left(p_{1}r_{b}^{4}\left(-3+\frac{2r_{b}^{2}\iota_{0}^{2}}{R_{0}^{2}}\right)-24\psi(r_{b})\right)\Bigg]^{1/2}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\psi_{4}

\displaystyle=-3\left(\mu_{0}p_{1}+2\frac{\iota_{0}^{2}}{R_{0}^{2}}\psi_{2}\right)

(R.8b)

\displaystyle\psi_{6}

\displaystyle=15\left(\frac{4\mu_{0}p_{1}\iota_{0}^{2}}{R_{0}^{2}}-\mu_{0}p_{2}\psi_{2}+\frac{8\iota_{0}^{4}\psi_{2}}{R_{0}^{4}}-\frac{6\iota_{0}\iota_{2}\psi_{2}^{2}}{R_{0}^{2}}\right).

(R.8c)

We compare the resulting solution for
\psi
to a numerical solution of (
R.1
) using MATLAB’s bvp4c routine. The solution is computed for
r\in[0,1]
with a boundary condition of
\psi(0)=0
and
\psi(1)=\psi_{0}
. The same profiles are used as described in Section
6.3.1
. The axis expansion solution is matched with the numerical solution at
r_{b}=10^{-2}
. In Figure
R.1
we present a comparison between the numerical solution and axis expansion of
\psi(r)
. As expected, the error in the axis expansion to
\mathcal{O}(r^{p})
scales as
\sim|r-r_{b}|^{p+2}
as one moves away from
r=r_{b}
.

scales as
